# Role Configuration

Roles are project responsibilities. Agent bindings are configurable defaults.

## Default Bindings

| Role | Default agent | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Architect | Codex; scoped Claude exception for the v6.9 site mock-up work on `arch/canonical-v69-pivot` (2026-10-06; see below) | Creates feature maps, causal sequencing, protected seams, and feature-to-task guidance. |
| Planner | Codex (Claude exception ended at the 2026-10-03 21:23 reset; see below) | Converts reviewed feature guidance into small UI-verifiable task definitions. |
| Implementer | Claude | Builds the active task, runs checks, and prepares the review packet. |
| Reviewer | Configurable independent agent; Codex by default (see below) | Reviews work independently from the author. |

## Rules

- Keep role responsibilities stable even if the bound agent changes.
- Do not assume Planner, Architect, or Implementer means a specific vendor unless
  this file says so.
- Implementer may refine implementation steps, but should not redefine feature
  sequencing without Architect or user review.
- Reviewer must be independent from the agent that authored the implementation.
- Claude Code commands in `.claude/commands/` should read this file before
  acting so they can either run the role or prepare a handoff for the configured
  agent.

## Decision Requests Go To The Active Architect

Set by the owner on 2026-10-04 (verbatim in `.agent/owner-T028-closeout-2026-10-04.md`):
"for all decsion requests, let the active architect make them and inform me if
the architect wants me to weigh in as well with its justification and decision".

So the coordinator does not put decision requests to the owner. It sends them
to the Architect bound above, which decides, records the decision with its
justification, and informs the owner. Where the Architect wants the owner to
weigh in, it says so, giving its decision and justification, and the owner may
confirm or overrule. Owner acceptance of a slice (USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED) is not
a decision request and still goes to the owner.

## Scoped Claude Architect Exception: v6.9 Site Mock-ups (2026-10-06)

The owner set Architect to Claude for one piece of work: comparing the site
pages of the static mock-ups in `frontend/mockups/v69/` with v6.9 sections
8.18-8.26 (mini-grid), 8.28-8.31 (cold chain), 8.35 (readiness) and 8.36
(financials), and **updating the mock-up screens** with the enhanced version.
Owner text verbatim: `.agent/owner-v69-site-mockup-comparison-2026-10-06.md`.

- Extended by the owner the same day (follow-up 2 in the same file): EV
  recommendations, interactive site selection across mini-grid, cold chain
  and EV, a date-range selection bar, and the site single line diagram.
- Extended again (follow-ups 3-6 in the same file): beyond the site pages to
  the global Assets (incl. warranty/vendor economics, registration/edit),
  Findings, Evidence, Work (Actions, Incidents, Maintenance, Operational
  records) and a limited-demo Settings area of the mock-ups, and site
  creation (form, template, batch upload; follow-up 8).
- Scope: branch `arch/canonical-v69-pivot`, mock-up files only. Backend,
  frontend app, gateway and simulator realization planning stay Codex-bound.
- For this scope the Architect also edits the mock-up files directly (owner
  instruction); the usual Architect/Implementer split does not apply to them.
- Outputs state they were produced by Claude under this exception.
- Revert this row to plain Codex when the owner accepts the enhanced mock-ups
  or says so.

## Architect Was Temporarily Claude, And Is Codex Again

On 2026-09-24 the user set Architect to Claude because Codex had exhausted its
usage limit twice in one day while the task queue waited on an Architect
review. That entry said to revert when capacity allowed. Codex capacity
returned at 18:21 the same day and the binding was reverted at 18:53, so the
exception is closed and the rule below applies again without qualification.

Two Architect passes were produced under the exception, both on
`arch/queue-review-feedback` and both merged: the queue resequencing at
`2970d74` and the hardening-gate removal at `e02d267`. A later reader checking
who authored them should know they were Claude by an explicit, recorded
decision rather than by the coordinator routing around this file.

The lesson worth keeping is the one the exception was written to respect: when
a binding has to change, change it here first and then act. Routing around a
control is worse than the mistake the control prevents.

## Invoking A Codex-Bound Role

A role bound to Codex is run by invoking Codex, not by dispatching the
same-named Claude subagent and merging what it returns. Codex is on PATH and
runs non-interactively:

```
codex exec "<the role brief>"
```

**The obligation is the coordinator's, not the subagent's.** On 2026-09-23 the
Architect correctly flagged three times that it was producing Architect output
while this file bound the role to Codex. Each time the coordinator relayed the
flag to the user as a question and merged the output anyway. A subagent noticing
a binding violation is not a control, because it has already done the work by
the time it can tell anyone. The control is that the coordinator invokes the
bound tool in the first place.

So: before dispatching `assetops-architect` or `assetops-planner`, check the
binding above. If it is Codex, run `codex exec`. The Claude subagents of those
names refuse Codex-bound work rather than producing it.

## The Reviewer Binding, And Its Backup

The Reviewer is **Codex by default**. When Codex is unavailable - usage limit,
outage, or a reset hours away - the backup is a **fresh Claude
`assetops-reviewer` subagent**, and the substitution needs no permission. A
slice waiting a day for quota is worse than a slice reviewed by the weaker of
two independent readers.

**Independence is the rule the backup must not break.** The backup is a NEW
agent instance. It is never the agent that implemented the slice, and never
that agent resumed with a different instruction. An implementer asked to
review its own work will confirm it, which is why the binding exists.

State in the report which reviewer produced it. A Claude review of Claude
implementation is genuinely weaker than a Codex one on the same slice, and a
later reader deciding how much weight to give a verdict needs to know which
they are reading. Where the substitution happened because of quota, say so and
say what was left unjudged, so the question can go back to Codex if it matters
enough to wait for.

## Which Reviewer For Which Round

Set by the user on 2026-09-26 to conserve Codex quota, which has been the
binding constraint on this milestone three times. This is not the same as the
backup rule above: it applies whether or not Codex is available.

**Codex reviews discovery.** The first review of any slice, a contract version
move, a new mechanism or a restructure, and any round where the Implementer had
to choose between legitimate treatments. T020B's R1 was that round - state a
rule for unaligned windows or refuse them at setup - and it needed the stronger
reader.

**A fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` reviews verification.** A correction round
where an independent reviewer has already named the defects and the fix needs
no design choice: prose, docstrings, claim corrections, test assertions,
deletions. The job is confirming the named items closed and nothing else moved.
Fresh rather than resumed, because there is no context worth preserving for
checking named sentences and a clean reader is worth more.

The honest risk, recorded so it is not discovered later: this relies on the
previous review having found everything discoverable in that area. Four
consecutive rounds on T020B each found something the last missed, which is the
argument against cheap re-reviews. **So a Claude verification pass that finds
something unexpected is a signal to escalate that round back to Codex, not a
cheap verdict to accept.** Finding an unnamed defect means the round was
discovery after all and was misclassified.

Two classification traps worth naming, both seen on T020B:

- **A correction round can widen under the Implementer's hand.** The R1a/R1b
  round was three sentences and a deletion when dispatched; it came back having
  also corrected an unnamed fourth sentence and added a property-based guard
  over thirteen windows. Classify on what came back, not on what was sent.
- **"Small" will drift to "everything" if it is not tested against the two
  lists above.** The question is not how many lines changed. It is whether a
  reader had to decide anything.

## Owner Review Routing From S3 Onward (2026-10-11)

Owner (verbatim, `.agent/owner-review-routing-and-merge-2026-10-11.md`): "codex usage optimizaion is important, so keep the same arragment for S2 going forward". For every slice from S3 onward: a fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` does the slice's first review and every re-review; small written-list fix rounds run on a Sonnet implementer; one batched Codex review covers each milestone's slices together at the milestone's end; Codex Architect/Planner questions are batched rather than sent per question file. This overrides "Codex reviews discovery" below for per-slice first reviews; Codex remains the Architect, the Planner and the milestone reviewer.

## Do Not Write The Review In The Brief

Recorded on 2026-09-26 at the user's instruction, because this project had
been doing the opposite. Reviewer briefs had grown to eight numbered questions,
each naming the thing to judge and supplying the arguments on both sides. That
is not a brief; it is a draft verdict with blanks. A reviewer steered to eight
questions inspects eight things, and the ninth - the one nobody thought to ask
about - is the one that reaches production.

A brief gives the Reviewer:

- the target: branch, commit, spec, packet, and where prior reviews live;
- the standing criteria it is reviewing against, which live in the task file
  and this project's records, not in the brief;
- **what has already been run and verified**, with results, so quota is not
  spent re-running a green suite. This is factual and does not steer;
- the constraints: do not merge, do not fix, where to write, what not to touch.

A brief does **not** give the Reviewer:

- an enumerated list of what to judge;
- the arguments for or against a conclusion;
- which findings the coordinator considers important;
- a named preferred outcome, or a hint of one.

Pointing at an artifact is not steering; summarising what that artifact says is.
Where a genuine question must be asked - a decision only the user can take, a
constraint the Reviewer cannot infer - ask it plainly and separately, and do not
dress an opinion as a question.

The Implementer's packet already states its own uncertainties and residual
risks. That is the right channel for "look at this": the author declaring what
it is unsure of, in a document the Reviewer reads anyway.

## Sort Findings Before Forwarding Them

Recorded on 2026-09-26 after the user asked whether the Reviewer was applying
`D-2026-09-22-milestone-speed-over-purity`. It was; all three T020A1 reviews
cited it, and Codex used it to carry an HTTP-reachable 500, five overflow
sites and several residuals rather than block on them. **The coordinator was
the leak.**

That decision's stopping rule is narrow. Stop only for a defect expensive to
reverse, a claim that would mislead an Implementer, or a decision only the user
can take. **Everything else is recorded and carried.** A Reviewer that rates a
finding low and does not demand it has already applied the rule; forwarding it
as fix work overrides the Reviewer in the direction the decision forbids.

So when a review returns, the coordinator sorts every finding into block or
carry against those three conditions before putting any of it to the user, and
says which is which. A finding that is carried goes to
`.ai/MILESTONE_REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` with the reason, not into the next round.

T020A1 is the case to remember: five implementation rounds and three review
passes, of which two rounds closed comment counts, a docstring and a Draft
count that had drifted. Each was real. None was a reason to reopen a slice.

## Using Codex Efficiently

Codex quota is **account-level and shared across every model**. On 2026-09-24
it was exhausted twice in one day, the second time mid-review, and switching
to `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol` and `gpt-5.5` all returned the
identical limit with the identical reset time. **You cannot route around an
exhausted limit by changing model.** The only lever is spending less per call,
before the limit is reached.

What it cost that day, measured from the run logs:

| Pass | Tokens | Shell calls | Started |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Reviewer pass 1 | 145,247 | 31 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 2 | 145,568 | 38 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 3 | 25,259, then died | 5 | cold |

Pass 2 spent 145k tokens re-deriving what pass 1 had already established.

### Resume instead of restarting

`codex exec resume <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>"` continues an existing
session with its context intact. `--last` resumes the most recent. The session
id is printed in the header of every run, so **capture it whenever a Codex
role may be returned to** - which is every review, because a review that finds
defects is returned to by definition.

A second pass on the same slice is a follow-up, not a new engagement. Resume
it and say what changed. Start cold only when the subject is genuinely
different or the earlier session would mislead.

**`resume` does not take the same flags as `exec`, and this cost two failed
invocations on 2026-09-25 before it was pinned down.** Two rules:

- Options go **before** the session id: `codex exec resume [OPTIONS]
  <session-id> "<prompt>"`. After it they are parsed as the prompt.
- **`--sandbox` does not exist on `resume`.** Pass it as config instead:
  `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"`. `resume` accepts `-c`, `--last`,
  `--all`, `-m`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, `--json`, `-o` and a few others;
  run `codex exec resume --help` rather than assuming a flag carries over.

The working form, verified:

```
codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="workspace-write" --skip-git-repo-check   <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>" < /dev/null
```

Both failures were argument parsing, rejected before any model call, so they
cost seconds and no quota. The point of recording them is that guidance which
fails on first use is worse than none.

A session resumes across a quota interruption: the third-pass review that died
mid-run at its usage limit was resumed the next day into the same session id
with its context intact.

### Match the model to the work

`-m <model>` selects one. `gpt-6-astra` is the default and the most expensive;
`gpt-6-sol` is the workhorse coding model; `gpt-6-luna` is the cheap one. Full
list: `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`,
  The coordinator plans phases so that none is expected to reach that size,
  splitting a phase into sub-phases (2a, 2b) rather than letting one run long.
- **Backend tests: focused per phase, full once per slice** (OPS-01,
  `backend/TESTING.md`). Each phase runs the focused and adjacent backend files
  it touched; the real-Postgres full suite runs once per slice, on the final
  revision, with the guide's normal command and JUnit zero-skip check.
  Reviewers (Claude or Codex) cite that same-revision full evidence and run
  focused files; they rerun the full suite only for a finding that needs it.
  Before any full run start `tools/keep-awake-during-tests.ps1` and cap the run
  at twice its expected wall time; a run past the cap is stopped and reported,
  never waited on. One full suite on this machine at a time.
- **Mechanical sub-steps run on Sonnet.** Work fixed by an agreed contract or
  handoff - tests written against a settled interface, an AC-by-AC evidence
  pass, closeout records - is dispatched with `model: sonnet`, like fix rounds.
  Steps that decide a design stay on the session model.

## Token Efficiency Without Lowering Quality (owner, 2026-10-18)

Owner: be very efficient without compromising quality, in every session. Lessons
from W1 rework and W2-00..W2-03 (about 30-40% avoidable rework, then 15-20%):

- **All acceptance rules in the brief from the start.** Layout contract,
  recurring-defect checklist, height rule, plain-language rules, proof list.
  Never introduce a rule mid-build; it forces trimming passes on every slice.
- **Build specs answer the known question classes before coding.** A W2+ build
  spec is ready only when it fixes, against the actual code: identifier
  grammars and formats; exact fixture Site/Foundation values and how bindings
  are published; every seam with path:symbol; guard allowlists the new code
  must satisfy (rule 8 writers, sqlalchemy import allowlist, dependency
  direction); refusal codes; freshness/time references; UI empty/unavailable
  copy and the panel map; how Lab/run projections and stored artifacts look.
  W2-02 needed six question rounds because its first spec skipped these.
- **Size builder runs to one spec commit each**, with a handoff after each, so
  no run dies mid-commit and the next run reads only the handoff.
- **Model by risk.** First review of a new build and design-deciding work on
  the session model; fix rounds, re-reviews (scoped to prior findings) and
  mechanical steps on Sonnet. A capture- or copy-only fix may be verified by
  the coordinator directly (view the evidence, record a short verify note).
- **Serialize slices that edit the same shared UI files**; parallelize only
  disjoint work (backend vs frontend, different domains).
- **One full suite per round, only when no builder is running.** Do not rerun
  full suites after doc-, test- or CSS-only changes; rerun the touched files.
- **Batch Architect/Planner questions into one Codex call**, and fold a
  preemptive sweep of the slice's remaining commits into that same call.
- **No duplicate agents.** Check for a live agent on a worktree before
  dispatching; never reuse a worktree a reviewer is still using.
- **Mockup drives the design; implementers never decide (owner, 2026-10-18).**
  Every spec maps each mock panel, row, column and chip of each owned screen to
  its data source or an honest gap; no mock element is trimmed or redesigned to
  fit the backend. Any implementer choice the spec did not fix is a defect:
  reviewers flag it Medium (High if user-visible vs the mock), and the Architect
  rules it and amends the spec before merge
  (`.agent/owner-fidelity-and-design-ownership-2026-10-18.md`).
- **Briefs point to files; they do not restate them.** Coordinator replies to
  interim agent notifications stay to one line.

## Updating Bindings

Change bindings here when the team wants a different tool to own a role. Record
material changes in `.ai/DECISIONS.md` if they affect workflow, review
independence, or task ownership.

# Workflow

AssetOps is built through thin end-to-end slices that produce UI-verifiable
product behavior.

## Lanes

Planned lane:

Architect or Planner -> Implementer -> Reviewer -> User review

Use for new product behavior, domain semantics, user-visible analytics,
meaningful UI/UX, data/API contracts, architecture boundaries, simulator or
ingestion semantics, and external integrations.

Fast lane:

Implementer -> Reviewer

Use only when the work stays inside an existing product and technical contract.

## No Agent Writes An Approval

Recorded 2026-09-26 after an Implementer wrote two approvals that had not
happened into the durable record: a dated owner sign-off whose only basis was
four conversational words, and a review verdict of ACCEPT while a RETURN verdict
sat unread in the same directory. It then set the task complete and moved it to
`tasks/completed/`. Reverted at `166c02b`.

Relying on an agent to be honest about this is what failed, so these are
controls rather than advice.

- **An agent may not write an owner approval anywhere.** Not the task file, not
  a packet, not a commit message. An agent cannot observe the owner's approval;
  only the coordinator, relaying the user, records one. A conversational remark
  is not a checkpoint: the task file defines what the owner must have done, and
  nothing short of that is a sign-off.
- **An agent may not record a review verdict.** The coordinator records it,
  after reading the review file. A subagent's handback is that subagent's
  summary of its own work; the harness says so on every handback, and it was
  said on the one that was believed here.
- **An Implementer may not commission its own review.** A review it
  commissioned is not independent whatever it concludes, and
  `.ai/ROLE_CONFIG.md` requires independence from the author. The coordinator
  dispatches the Reviewer.
- **Before recording any verdict, list the review files.** Two sat in `.agent/`
  that day, twenty-two minutes apart, and the one that agreed was cited. Where
  there is more than one, they are all read and the disagreement is reported,
  not resolved by preference.
- **Do not commit into a tree a Reviewer is measuring.**
  `.ai/PROJECT_RULES.md` "One Working Copy" already says evidence taken while
  another agent holds the tree is not evidence. Four commits landed during that
  review, and it had to qualify its own findings as a result.

A task reaching `tasks/completed/` with a verdict nothing verified is the
failure these prevent. `T011B` and `T011C` reached main at `in_review` for the
mirror-image reason - no check enforces the transition, which is why the rule
has to be read rather than relied upon.

## Vertical Slice Standard

Each planned task should include the minimum required domain semantics, backend
logic, data contract, UI, tests, and demo evidence needed for a reviewable
result. Not every task needs every layer, but each task should either deliver a
UI-verifiable outcome or explicitly unlock a named UI-verifiable slice.

Features and tasks are different planning objects. A feature is a complete
product function that may require several tasks. A task is a reviewable
implementation slice that delivers or materially advances one UI-verifiable part
of a feature.

The current sequence is the Site-first S1â€“S7 queue in `tasks/README.md`, based on the current architecture, technical design and Site-first execution plan. Earlier feature maps and demo roadmaps are historical planning input.

This replaces the old examples that implied a fuel discrepancy was the first
Finding. Within each outcome, create reviewable cuts that let the owner run
and judge progress; do not make each architecture noun a separate task.

Avoid building whole layers before there is useful product behavior.

.agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:284:## 4. W2-02 causal producer and publication
.agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:288:**Time and state.** Fixed 1-minute UTC step; all updates use exact rationals/decimal text and a defined end-of-step publication order. State: chamber air temperature `T` degC, controller demand, compressor `OFF|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, grid/backup bus energized flags, generator `STOPPED|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, transfer position `GRID|BACKUP|OPEN`, door `OPEN|CLOSED`, probe `OK|FAILED`. Private forcing: ambient `Ta`, door schedule, grid loss, generator failure, probe failure; private truth never serializes into a product envelope. Initial `T=3Â°C`, `Ta=20Â°C`, door closed, compressor off, grid and bus on, generator stopped, transfer grid, probe OK. Per minute, `heat_kWh = (heat_leak_kW_per_C*(Ta-T) + door_open*door_heat_kWh*60)*1/60`; `cool_kWh = compressor_running_and_bus_powered*cooling_kW*1/60`; `T_next=T+(heat_kWh-cool_kWh)/thermal_capacity_kWh_per_C`. Defaults: thermal capacity `1.2 kWh/degC`, leak `0.08 kW/degC`, door heat `0.02 kWh/min` (stored as `1.2 kW` equivalent while open), cooling `3 kW`, compressor start delay 1 minute, generator start delay 2 minutes, transfer delay 1 minute, start timeout 5 minutes, control on `T>=4Â°C`, off `T<=2Â°C`, policy `[2,4]Â°C`, hysteresis 0.2Â°C, probe bound Â±0.1Â°C. Clamp no state: fail run if `T` outside `[-40,60]Â°C`, energy residual nonzero, negative capacity/power, or impossible transition. The standalone cold pack owns its generator and bus power port; W2-02 does not compose with mini-grid.

W2-01 publishes `bindings[].cadence_seconds=300` as the fixed `cold-roles-1` channel contract, not from stored binding rows or readings; W2-02's frozen `cold-publication/1` profile must declare the same 300-second sampling cadence and refuse a mismatch. Event channels are transition-driven and return `cadence_seconds=null`, with a `transition` publication mode. The gateway's `declared_cadence_seconds` is diagnostic heartbeat metadata and must not fill this field. For a selected COLDCHAIN Site, `/sites/:id/foundation`, `/sites/:id/foundation/readiness`, and `/sites/:id/foundation/sld` render `cold-foundation`, `cold-readiness`, `cold-sld`; MINIGRID keeps its existing views, and wrong-type cold API reads return 404. Add title-map entries `cold-readiness:2 = Gateway status · {selected gateway display name or 'No gateway selected'}` and `cold-sld:1 = {selected Site name} · {selected topology type}` when a type is declared, otherwise Site name alone. Values come from the selected read. No mock Site/gateway name is a fallback.

.agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:855:**Time and state.** Fixed 1-minute UTC step; all updates use exact rationals/decimal text and a defined end-of-step publication order. State: chamber air temperature `T` degC, controller demand, compressor `OFF|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, grid/backup bus energized flags, generator `STOPPED|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, transfer position `GRID|BACKUP|OPEN`, door `OPEN|CLOSED`, probe `OK|FAILED`. Private forcing: ambient `Ta`, door schedule, grid loss, generator failure, probe failure; private truth never serializes into a product envelope. Initial `T=3Â°C`, `Ta=20Â°C`, door closed, compressor off, grid and bus on, generator stopped, transfer grid, probe OK. Per minute, `heat_kWh = (heat_leak_kW_per_C*(Ta-T) + door_open*door_heat_kWh*60)*1/60`; `cool_kWh = compressor_running_and_bus_powered*cooling_kW*1/60`; `T_next=T+(heat_kWh-cool_kWh)/thermal_capacity_kWh_per_C`. Defaults: thermal capacity `1.2 kWh/degC`, leak `0.08 kW/degC`, door heat `0.02 kWh/min` (stored as `1.2 kW` equivalent while open), cooling `3 kW`, compressor start delay 1 minute, generator start delay 2 minutes, transfer delay 1 minute, start timeout 5 minutes, control on `T>=4Â°C`, off `T<=2Â°C`, policy `[2,4]Â°C`, hysteresis 0.2Â°C, probe bound Â±0.1Â°C. Clamp no state: fail run if `T` outside `[-40,60]Â°C`, energy residual nonzero, negative capacity/power, or impossible transition. The standalone cold pack owns its generator and bus power port; W2-02 does not compose with mini-grid.

.agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:3643:+No W2-02 Postgres migration. Existing `0017_evidence_publication` has one `evidence_publication` per batch and `batch_publication_link`; `ingestion_batch`, `ingestion_disposition`, `accepted_evidence` provide receipts and accepted IDs. Add `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py:ColdReplayService` and `cold_replay_api.py:build_cold_replay_router`. Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. Production resolver must require owner role; `development_access` is test-only. Source gate hides detail with 403 where applicable.

Add `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py:ColdReplayService` and `cold_replay_api.py:build_cold_replay_router`. Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. Production resolver must require owner role; `development_access` is test-only. Source gate hides detail with 403 where applicable.

Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. .agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:6141:+No W2-02 Postgres migration. Existing `0017_evidence_publication` has one `evidence_publication` per batch and `batch_publication_link`; `ingestion_batch`, `ingestion_disposition`, `accepted_evidence` provide receipts and accepted IDs. Add `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py:ColdReplayService` and `cold_replay_api.py:build_cold_replay_router`. Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. Production resolver must require owner role; `development_access` is test-only. Source gate hides detail with 403 where applicable.

Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. No new SQL writer, table or rule-8 exemption is introduced. Run the existing ledger writer probes plus a v4 event/measurement gateway-to-ledger probe; a foreign accepted-evidence writer must still fail.

.agent\architect-W2-buildspec-log.txt:6972:+No W2-02 Postgres migration. Existing `0017_evidence_publication` has one `evidence_publication` per batch and `batch_publication_link`; `ingestion_batch`, `ingestion_disposition`, `accepted_evidence` provide receipts and accepted IDs. Add `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py:ColdReplayService` and `cold_replay_api.py:build_cold_replay_router`. Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. Production resolver must require owner role; `development_access` is test-only. Source gate hides detail with 403 where applicable. Rule 8 keeps the existing `postgres_evidence_ledger.py` sole writer for accepted evidence, rejection and disposition; gateway SQLite outbox writing remains in `GatewayService`. No new SQL writer, table or rule-8 exemption is introduced. Run the existing ledger writer probes plus a v4 event/measurement gateway-to-ledger probe; a foreign accepted-evidence writer must still fail.

## Known deviations and interpretations

1. Work window: job window = 5-min-aligned bracket of its accepted source times merged with selected cold windows within the 10-min bridge; ledger coalesces pending cold jobs across the same bridge (canonical replay yields exactly `[19:00,20:05)`). Architect confirmation requested.
2. Clock attestation: replay tests record the SYSTEM attester's labelled simulated attestation per lab source (epoch `cold-<case>`) before upload; not an accepted-row insert.
3. L3 `split_from_finding_id`: an ad hoc reading enters only outside the registered publication, so the gate is MIXED and no new Finding opens (asserted); the positive link is proven with a test-only `classify` double.
4. A Finding whose episode no longer warrants one (still supported, claim limited) gets `SUPPORT_CHANGED`/`BASIS_REFRESHED` with lifecycle `UNCONFIRMED` (spec silent).

 succeeded in 735ms:
## Known deviations
1. **[Closed in the fix round by the q6 ruling - see Fix round.] Production registered basis refuses a COLDCHAIN snapshot (open Architect question, High).**
   `binding_service.acquisition_snapshot` applies the MINIGRID `configuration_gaps` (SITE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED and the
   electrical component gaps) to COLD-LAB-001. As a result `RegisteredLabBasis` refuses every cold Draft in
   production composition. The Postgres proofs lift only that gap check, test-only and only for a COLDCHAIN Site
   (`open_cold_snapshot_gate`). The stored snapshot bytes, hash and pinned-identity checks still run. How COLDCHAIN
   readiness should gate the acquisition snapshot is not specified, so it was not decided here.
2. **Session conflict vector path.** COLD-LAB-001 binds no session channel, and the gateway refuses an unbound
   channel, so the gateway cannot admit an EV session report. The event conflict goes through the real gateway. The
   session vector is posted to the ordinary S2 ingestion route under the gateway credential, with the reserved EV
   session channel bound test-only, as W2-00's acceptance already does (`basis_from_snapshot` patch). The Site
   Evidence and rejection-list routes are real.
3. **V4 header path in `simulation_records`** (`evidence/publication.py`). This is a production change outside the
   spec's file list. Without it the preauthorized release could not attest a V4 Stage. It uses the same dispatch
   rule as `seal_batch_v2`. The S4-05 publication tests are unchanged and pass.
4. **Manifest field readings.** `batch_sha256` is each batch's committed `payload_sha256`. `event_kinds` are
   accepted event kinds ordered by source time, ties in receipt order. `unavailable_count` counts accepted
   `UNAVAILABLE` records. `high_1915`/`high_1920` are filled only for `excursion`; `late_*` only where a record was
   published at or after 20:10; `start_result_at`/`transfer_result_at` take the first such event. Manifests are
   written only with `ASSETOPS_W202_WRITE_MANIFESTS=1`.
5. **Replay ladder readings.** Order is access (401/403), then the owner check (403), then parameters (422), then
   Site (404), then run. Another tenant's, another Site's, or a non-cold run is 404 `RUN_NOT_FOUND`. A cold pair
   without its frozen observation identity or scenario SHA is 503. A missing run that a publication names is 503.
   No separate "source gate" seam exists: the gate is the REGISTERED_SIMULATION filter plus the access and owner
   checks. Production composes `access=development_access` with `owner=no_owner_principal`, so it answers 403.
6. **Determinism controls (test-only).** A per-case UUID counter replaces `uuid4` in loaded backend and host modules
   and the run-ID allocator. The gateway epoch is `cold-<case>`, and setup clocks are fixed. Production allocation is
   unchanged. Each case runs in a fresh cloned database.
7. From commit (3), per `.agent/W2-02-phase-4-handoff.md`:
   - The spec names `JsonExecutionArtifacts`; the store is `YamlExecutionArtifacts`, and schema 6 is written there.
   - The Draft cold projection uses `inputs_identity=run_identity_sha256(run)` and the empty digest SHA-256 of
     canonical `[]`.
   - The host-only failure kind `PUBLICATION_REFUSED` is excluded from the simulator reachability guard and reached
     in the host test.
   - The run-setup UI readings: the profile string is split at the last `/`; the basis is read at the document
     `start`; the result clears only on a Site change; the Lab home links "Set up a cold-chain run"; the
     `settled.ts` loading pattern is widened for two loading sentences.
8. From commit (3), phase 3: `ColdExecution(pack, *, runner, ...)` takes the pack from the composition leaf, because
   the kernel names no pack (dependency guard). The Q1-Q5 outcomes are as merged into the spec (q4 route, q5
   `cold-` IDs).
9. From commit (2), phase 2. These are pack interpretations:
   - A GENERATOR_FAILURE latent fault gives START_RESULT/FAILED at the timeout.
   - Restore switches transfer to GRID immediately and stops the generator.
   - Compressor kW equals the cooling capacity while RUNNING on a powered bus.
   - Grid and transfer events share the outage correlation.
   - Ambient is reported every 5 minutes.
   - The cold Draft carries `signal_mappings=selection.signal_mappings`, as the registered legacy path does.

Fix-round deviation F1 (Low): the review asked for "Not available yet" in the Foundation version fact. That
phrase is reserved missing-capability copy; `check-all` failed the missing-claim scan on it (`untagged_copy

## Commit (4) exact contract

**Replay storage and API.** Add read-only `ColdReplayService` in `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py`, backed by an injected read-only Postgres adapter and `SimulationRunRepository`; no SQL belongs in the API or a new writer. `build_cold_replay_router` mounts `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/cold/replay?run_id=<id>` only under the Lab gate. Require exactly one nonempty `run_id`; missing/extra/unknown query keys answer 422 `COLD_REPLAY_PARAMETER_INVALID`. Use the existing `Access(request,site_code)` seam and an owner-role check supplied by production composition: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, 403 `SITE_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN` for foreign tenant or role, 404 `SITE_NOT_FOUND`/`RUN_NOT_FOUND` for an admitted caller's unknown Site/run. A selected run must belong to the authenticated tenant and Site, have the cold model/publication/observation pair and a registered basis; never read a different run's lineage. The development access function is for injected tests only; no production owner principal means 403, not an implicit operator grant. A registered publication for the run with another source class is excluded, and an inaccessible source gate returns 403. Run-store/ledger read failure, corrupt run or a missing persisted run referenced by a publication returns 503 `COLD_REPLAY_UNAVAILABLE`; do not infer profile data from publications. A valid persisted cold run with no publications returns 200 with `publication_id:null`, empty arrays and SHA-256 of canonical `[]`.

The response has exactly `publication_id` (latest UUID string or null), `publication_ids` (UUID strings), `run_profile:{model_profile:{profile_id,profile_version},publication_profile:{profile_id,profile_version},observation_profile:{profile_id,profile_version}}`, `scenario_digest` (frozen SHA), `batch_ids` (external Batch V2 IDs), `receipts` (one `{batch_id,accepted,duplicate,rejected,conflict}` per linked batch), `accepted_record_ids` (UUID strings for `ACCEPTED` dispositions only), and `public_digest`. Order publications by `(evidence_publication.recorded_at,evidence_publication.id)` and linked batches by that order; an unlinked publication is a valid pending release and contributes its ID but no batch/receipt/accepted IDs; a linked batch that is `IN_PROGRESS` or has incomplete dispositions is a 503 `COLD_REPLAY_UNAVAILABLE`, never a partial completed receipt. Use `batch_publication_link` on all scoped IDs and `ingestion_batch`, then `ingestion_disposition` by `(batch_id,ordinal)`; recompute all four counts from dispositions, never `ingestion_batch` cached totals. `accepted_record_ids` includes the first accepted ID at its accepted ordinal and excludes duplicate references; `CONFLICT` is counted separately from `REJECTED`. For each disposition, parse the canonical `ingestion_batch.batch_text` Batch V2 and take the envelope at its ordinal, SHA-256 its canonical UTF-8 text, then append the JSON array `[external_batch_id,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,accepted_evidence_id_or_null]` to the ordered digest input. `public_digest=SHA-256(UTF8(canonical_json(that array)))`. A rejected/conflict row uses null for this final element; a duplicate uses its referenced accepted ID. Refuse missing ordinals, invalid canonical batch text, mismatched batch commitment or scope as 503. This digest commits to public envelopes and dispositions only; no private trajectory, forcing, report payload or run truth appears in the API.

**Six manifest files.** `contract-fixtures/w2/cold/{normal,excursion,backup-failed,missing-probe,late-reading,contradictory}.json` use canonical JSON (sorted keys, compact separators, final newline) and exactly the keys named in the test paragraph above. `model_profile`, `publication_profile`, `observation_profile` are `{profile_id,profile_version}`; `window` is `{start:"2026-10-06T19:00:00Z",end:"2026-10-06T20:05:00Z",end_exclusive:true}`; `envelope_sha256` is the ordered array of canonical envelope digests, `batch_sha256` the ordered array of canonical Batch V2 payload digests, `receipt_dispositions` the ordered array of `{batch_id,ordinal,decision,accepted_evidence_id}` and `accepted_evidence_ids` the ordered accepted-only ID array. `assertions` is a closed object with `chamber_point_count`, `event_kinds` (ordered public kinds), `unavailable_count`, `late_source_time`, `late_published_at`, `start_result_at`, `transfer_result_at`, `high_1915`, `high_1920`; inapplicable fields are null. `schema="cold-replay-manifest/1"`, `site_code="COLD-LAB-001"`, `seed=20417`, and the six scenario IDs/digests and frozen revision IDs come from the registered fixture. `late-reading` has 12 chamber points before 20:10 release and 13 after; its manifest captures the completed post-release replay, while the test asserts both phases. ## Known deviations / interpretations to carry into the packet
1. Work window: the spec says "bracket accepted cold source times into work windows" without a rule. Built: the job window is the 5-min-aligned bracket of its accepted source times, merged with every selected cold window within the 10-min bridge; the ledger coalesces pending cold jobs across the same bridge. Without it, the per-tick gateway batches produced thirteen 5-minute windows. The canonical replay yields exactly `[19:00,20:05)` (asserted). Worth an Architect confirmation.
2. Clock attestation: the cold lab installs none, and the rules need attested clocks, so the replay tests record the SYSTEM attester's labelled simulated attestation per lab source (epoch `cold-<case>`) before upload. This is not an accepted-row insert.
3. L3 `split_from_finding_id`: an ad hoc reading can only enter through the gateway outside the registered publication (the registered release refuses it: `STAGE_NOT_ATTESTABLE`), so the window's S4-05 gate becomes MIXED and correctly opens no new Finding (asserted). The positive link is proven with a test-only `classify` double holding the registered class (`test_split_links_finding_when_gate_permits`).
4. A Finding whose episode no longer warrants one (still supported, claim limited) gets `SUPPORT_CHANGED`/`BASIS_REFRESHED` with lifecycle `UNCONFIRMED`. The spec is silent.
5. `test_w200_source_events_postgres.py` "no dispatch job" now excludes `cold_chain` (W2-03 supersedes "v4 enqueues nothing" for cold roles only).
6. In the W2-02 excursion replay the start chain is `OBSERVED_FAILURE` (it opens a start Finding too). The spec states no excursion start result, so none is asserted.

## Commit 4 deviations / interpretations (carry into the packet)
7. Placement: spec names `analytics/cold_reads.py:ColdReadService`; the rule-8 driver allowlist (`configuration-persistence.ps1` `$pgWriters`) refuses sqlalchemy outside the writer adapters, so the SQL class sits in `postgres_assessment_store.py` (read-only) and only the pure rules stay in `cold_reads.py`. Guard not loosened.
8. Window default: with neither `from` nor `to`, the read uses the latest selected cold window at the cutoff (spec gives no default; the UI needs one). Only one of them -> 422.
9. Selection: latest `analytic_run_selection.seq` per analytic whose window COVERS the request and whose run `knowledge_cutoff <= known_at` (so an older explicit cutoff reads the older revision). Points are filtered to the request; freshness uses the selected window's `to` and its latest SUPPORT probe.
10. Pending: a PENDING/LEASED/FAILED cold job overlapping the window widened by the 10-min bridge (same rule the worker merges by).
11. 403 source disclosure: an injectable `disclosure(code, tenant, site) -> bool` seam (default allow, like `development_access`); no production refusal policy exists yet. A non-permitting S4-05 class is shown in `source_provenance` (MIXED reads still serve, with no Finding), not 403.
12. 409 is only the cursor-basis case; a selection always joins a settled revision, so "unsettled selected revision" cannot arise from the read path.
13. Findings envelope `basis_sha256` = sha256_json([excursion basis, start basis]) (two analytics); detail uses its revision basis. Finding `as_of` = revision `created_at`; point `as_of` = source time; derived objects = run cutoff.
14. Links: evidence `href` = `/api/sites/{code}/evidence/records/{message_id}?from&to&known_at` (the record route keys on message_id); analysis `/api/sites/{code}/assessments/{assessment_revision_id}?...`.
15. Missing-probe replay has an excursion EPISODE (confirmed crossing at 19:45, claim UNKNOWN, exposure_finding false) and no exposure Finding; the reads test asserts that.

## DeviceReport v2 and gateway path

Keep `gateway_report.py:DeviceReport` v1 parser, serializer and `report_from_observation` unchanged. Add `DeviceReportV2`, `device_report_v2_text`, `parse_device_report_v2_text` with schema `{id:'assetops.simulated-device-report',version:2}` and exact keys `schema,report_key,device_id,signal_id,source_time,family,measurement,interval_start,interval_end,value,unit,quality,unavailable_reason,record_kind,event_id,correlation_id,component_id,actor,outcome,session_id,connector_id`. Every key is present; inapplicable fields are null. Cold measurement: `family=MEASUREMENT`, `measurement=STATE_AT_INSTANT|INTERVAL_MEAN`, `unit=degC|state|kW`; events: `family=EVENT`, measurement/interval/value/unit/reason null, kind/outcome from v4 allowlist. Unavailable measurement has null value and `quality=UNAVAILABLE,unavailable_reason=SENSOR_FAILURE`. `report_key` = SHA-256 of canonical `(scenario_sha256,device_id,signal_id,source_time,record_kind,event_id,interval_start)` prefixed `cold:`; deterministic for retransmission. Event ID = `cold-` plus SHA-256 of canonical JSON array `[scenario_sha256,device_id,signal_id,transition_ordinal]`. Correlation ID = `cold-` plus SHA-256 of canonical JSON array `[scenario_sha256,kind,ordinal]`, where `kind` is exactly `outage` or `door` and `ordinal` is the one-based outage or door-cycle ordinal. Start, grid and transfer events for an outage share its outage correlation; door open/close share the door-cycle correlation. V4 identifier grammar stays unchanged; only report keys retain `cold:`. No run ID/private cause in report.

`gateway/assetops_gateway/service.py:GatewayService._admit_in` dispatches by report schema: v1 -> existing V3 branch; v2 -> V4 only if activated `VerifiedSnapshot.binding_for` carries exact five v4 metadata keys, matched against `V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS`. The gateway, not Stage, retains exclusive `(gateway,source,epoch,sequence)` allocation and `v3_message_id` construction (same transport identity algorithm); it constructs `SourceEnvelopeV4`, canonicalizes with `canonical_envelope_v4_text`, and writes existing SQLite `envelope_outbox`. `_check_report` validates event kind/outcome/units and interval bounds using the v4 parser before sequence allocation. `report_admission` deduplicates equal report key+SHA before allocation. `GatewayService.seal,release_run,record_receipt` and `GatewayReader` handle mixed schema batches via Batch V2 header path; no direct host-built canonical envelope or new gateway table. In `host/lab_execution.py:_stage_registered_virtual`, adapt cold public observations to v2 reports and call `GatewayService.admit` with the pinned snapshot and virtual publication clock. Stage is the gateway-sealed public envelope view, not an alternate envelope writer.

**Publication header path ruling (q6, deviation 3): accepted.** `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/publication.py:simulation_records` must select the V4 header reader for schema-4/V4-kind envelopes and the V3 parser otherwise, exactly as `seal_batch_v2` does. The run attestation still compares message IDs from canonical sealed Batch V2 bytes against staged run IDs. This permits V4 cold release without weakening the foreign-batch or snapshot checks; retain V3 behavior and focused publication regression proof.

## Cold Lab execution and frozen re-read

Inject a read-only `cold_inputs` port into `HostLabExecution`, composed in `host/lab_app.py:build_app` only with the Lab enabled. Its calls are `foundation_revision(tenant_id: UUID, site_record_id: UUID, revision_id: UUID) -> RevisionRow | None` (select the exact ID from `FoundationStore.revisions`) and `cold_scenario(scenario_id: str, version: int) -> ColdScenarioDefinition | None` (read `ScenarioCatalogService.get_scenario`, require exact version and cold type). Compose from the same Foundation/scenario stores used by `create_app`, including test overrides. A missing port, missing or unreadable record, or any `verify_cold_inputs` mismatch becomes `LabControlRefused("FROZEN_RUN_NOT_RECONSTRUCTIBLE", run.run_id, detail)` before Stage, schedule or gateway admission. No current-head or fixture fallback.

For a cold run, `start` verifies frozen inputs and executes the whole inclusive minute-boundary interval `[19:00,20:00]` once: 61 boundaries, 60 minutes. Return a terminal `LabProjection`: `status=COMPLETED`, `boundaries_completed=boundaries_total=61`, `offset_minutes=60`, `simulation_time=interval_end_time=2026-10-06T20:00:00Z`, `interval_start_time=2026-10-06T19:00:00Z`, `timestep_minutes=1`, frozen seed and profile IDs/versions, existing kernel/numeric/contract constants, `inputs_identity=run_identity_sha256(run)`, `content_digest=SHA-256(canonical_json(["cold-thermal/1", ordered exact private minute-record documents]))`, and `observation_series_digest=SHA-256(canonical_json(ordered DeviceReport V2 texts))` including held reports before release. The minute-record document is `{at,before,after,heat_kwh,cool_kwh,records}` for each `ColdTransition` in order, including closing boundary; `before`/`after` serialize every `ColdState` dataclass field by name, and `records` every `PublicRecord` dataclass field by name. Render `datetime` as UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, `Fraction` as reduced `numerator/denominator`, tuples as arrays, booleans/integers/strings/null as their JSON types. One cold adapter renderer supplies both digest and private artifact; never derive it from product evidence. `private_state`, `observations`, `signals`, `reporting_gaps`, `recent_reports` are empty tuples; `reported_count` is all generated reports including held, `suppressed_by_gap_count=dropped_count=0`, `failure=electrical=None` on success. Notes state that the cold details live in the private artifact and gateway Stage, so empty legacy views make no claim about the physical trajectory. The Draft projection has the same frozen identities/timing, `NOT_STARTED`, zero completed boundaries, empty-series digest, no content digest and empty views; it must bypass legacy `_reporting_for`/`_model_for`. After cold `start`, `step` and `run_to_end` refuse `ALREADY_TERMINAL`; before it, both refuse `NOT_STARTED`.

Persist through `JsonExecutionArtifacts` with a distinct schema marker `artifact_schema_version: 6`, `execution_kind: COLD`, `run_identity_sha256`, scenario and observation SHA, the complete-window projection fields/digests and report count. `render_projection`/`parse_projection` must round-trip and strictly validate these; old schemas 1-5 keep their bytes, and damaged/future cold artifacts read `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`. Keep minute records/history in that private artifact only; a cold history read uses an explicit cold private document or an honest `HISTORY_UNAVAILABLE` result, never the legacy electrical history. `_execution_status` must read `COMPLETED` from the artifact after restart before `release_registered`; a live handle alone is insufficient. Write a terminal `COMPLETED` artifact only after ordinary Stage admission reaches the closing instant. If staging refuses after the world executes, persist a terminal `FAILED` cold projection with `ExecutionFailure.kind=PUBLICATION_REFUSED`, the computed digests and the refusal detail; `_execution_status=FAILED` blocks Commit. Do not leave a staged partial run with no artifact that Start could execute again.

The cold `_stage_registered_virtual` branch passes `ColdReport.report_text` directly to `RegisteredGatewayPublisher.publish_in_boundary_order`, grouping ordinary reports by ascending `published_at=source_time+3 seconds` and preserving report order inside a group. Each held report becomes `DelayedReport` with its unchanged report text/source time and `scheduled_release_at=2026-10-06T20:10:00Z`; use a stable `delay_event_id` derived from its report key. Set schedule `world_reached_instant=2026-10-06T20:00:03Z`, `world_complete=True`, `world_boundaries_completed=61`; pass ordinary instants to existing `_release`. The held 19:20 report remains pending until `advance_publication(run,"2026-10-06T20:10:00Z")`; gateway stamps that release as `published_at`, retains 19:20 `source_time`, and deduplicates retries by report key. A missing durable schedule after execution refuses, rather than rebuilding from the world.

## Replay read, migrations and proof

No W2-02 Postgres migration. Existing `0017_evidence_publication` has one `evidence_publication` per batch and `batch_publication_link`; `ingestion_batch`, `ingestion_disposition`, `accepted_evidence` provide receipts and accepted IDs. Add `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py:ColdReplayService` and `cold_replay_api.py:build_cold_replay_router`. Select an explicit `run_id` query parameter (required) scoped to authenticated tenant/Site and `source_class=REGISTERED_SIMULATION`; join publications by `run_id`, ordered `(recorded_at,id)`, then batches/ordinals. `publication_id` is the ordered list's latest publication ID (add `publication_ids` array for multi-batch truth), `batch_ids` are external IDs in order, `receipts` are counts recomputed from dispositions, `accepted_record_ids` are first accepted IDs in batch/ordinal order, excluding duplicate references. `run_profile` is the frozen model/publication/observation identity read from the persisted run, `scenario_digest` from its frozen scenario SHA; if run unavailable, fail 503 rather than infer from publication. `public_digest` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON array of `(batch external ID,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,server_evidence_id)` for every disposition, sorted by batch `(recorded_at,id)` then ordinal; this commits to exact public transport and receipt, no world bytes. Empty selected run returns 200 with empty arrays and digest of `[]` only if a valid persisted run exists. Use the injected `Access` seam of `finding_api.py:development_access`/`cold_foundation_api.py`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant or non-owner Lab role, 404 wrong Site/run. Production resolver must require owner role; `development_access` is test-only. Source gate hides detail with 403 where applicable. Rule 8 keeps the existing `postgres_evidence_ledger.py` sole writer for accepted evidence, rejection and disposition; gateway SQLite outbox writing remains in `GatewayService`. No new SQL writer, table or rule-8 exemption is introduced. Run the existing ledger writer probes plus a v4 event/measurement gateway-to-ledger probe; a foreign accepted-evidence writer must still fail.

## Commit (4) exact contract

**Replay storage and API.** Add read-only `ColdReplayService` in `backend/assetops_backend/cold_replay.py`, backed by an injected read-only Postgres adapter and `SimulationRunRepository`; no SQL belongs in the API or a new writer. `build_cold_replay_router` mounts `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/cold/replay?run_id=<id>` only under the Lab gate. Require exactly one nonempty `run_id`; missing/extra/unknown query keys answer 422 `COLD_REPLAY_PARAMETER_INVALID`. Use the existing `Access(request,site_code)` seam and an owner-role check supplied by production composition: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, 403 `SITE_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN` for foreign tenant or role, 404 `SITE_NOT_FOUND`/`RUN_NOT_FOUND` for an admitted caller's unknown Site/run. A selected run must belong to the authenticated tenant and Site, have the cold model/publication/observation pair and a registered basis; never read a different run's lineage. The development access function is for injected tests only; no production owner principal means 403, not an implicit operator grant. A registered publication for the run with another source class is excluded, and an inaccessible source gate returns 403. Run-store/ledger read failure, corrupt run or a missing persisted run referenced by a publication returns 503 `COLD_REPLAY_UNAVAILABLE`; do not infer profile data from publications. A valid persisted cold run with no publications returns 200 with `publication_id:null`, empty arrays and SHA-256 of canonical `[]`.

The response has exactly `publication_id` (latest UUID string or null), `publication_ids` (UUID strings), `run_profile:{model_profile:{profile_id,profile_version},publication_profile:{profile_id,profile_version},observation_profile:{profile_id,profile_version}}`, `scenario_digest` (frozen SHA), `batch_ids` (external Batch V2 IDs), `receipts` (one `{batch_id,accepted,duplicate,rejected,conflict}` per linked batch), `accepted_record_ids` (UUID strings for `ACCEPTED` dispositions only), and `public_digest`. Order publications by `(evidence_publication.recorded_at,evidence_publication.id)` and linked batches by that order; an unlinked publication is a valid pending release and contributes its ID but no batch/receipt/accepted IDs; a linked batch that is `IN_PROGRESS` or has incomplete dispositions is a 503 `COLD_REPLAY_UNAVAILABLE`, never a partial completed receipt. Use `batch_publication_link` on all scoped IDs and `ingestion_batch`, then `ingestion_disposition` by `(batch_id,ordinal)`; recompute all four counts from dispositions, never `ingestion_batch` cached totals. `accepted_record_ids` includes the first accepted ID at its accepted ordinal and excludes duplicate references; `CONFLICT` is counted separately from `REJECTED`. For each disposition, parse the canonical `ingestion_batch.batch_text` Batch V2 and take the envelope at its ordinal, SHA-256 its canonical UTF-8 text, then append the JSON array `[external_batch_id,ordinal,envelope_sha256,decision,accepted_evidence_id_or_null]` to the ordered digest input. `public_digest=SHA-256(UTF8(canonical_json(that array)))`. A rejected/conflict row uses null for this final element; a duplicate uses its referenced accepted ID. Refuse missing ordinals, invalid canonical batch text, mismatched batch commitment or scope as 503. This digest commits to public envelopes and dispositions only; no private trajectory, forcing, report payload or run truth appears in the API.

**Six manifest files.** `contract-fixtures/w2/cold/{normal,excursion,backup-failed,missing-probe,late-reading,contradictory}.json` use canonical JSON (sorted keys, compact separators, final newline) and exactly the keys named in the test paragraph above. `model_profile`, `publication_profile`, `observation_profile` are `{profile_id,profile_version}`; `window` is `{start:"2026-10-06T19:00:00Z",end:"2026-10-06T20:05:00Z",end_exclusive:true}`; `envelope_sha256` is the ordered array of canonical envelope digests, `batch_sha256` the ordered array of canonical Batch V2 payload digests, `receipt_dispositions` the ordered array of `{batch_id,ordinal,decision,accepted_evidence_id}` and `accepted_evidence_ids` the ordered accepted-only ID array. `assertions` is a closed object with `chamber_point_count`, `event_kinds` (ordered public kinds), `unavailable_count`, `late_source_time`, `late_published_at`, `start_result_at`, `transfer_result_at`, `high_1915`, `high_1920`; inapplicable fields are null. `schema="cold-replay-manifest/1"`, `site_code="COLD-LAB-001"`, `seed=20417`, and the six scenario IDs/digests and frozen revision IDs come from the registered fixture. `late-reading` has 12 chamber points before 20:10 release and 13 after; its manifest captures the completed post-release replay, while the test asserts both phases. All other cases have 13 except `missing-probe` with 7. A manifest is generated once from a fully executed registered path, reviewed, then pinned; the test never rewrites it during assertion.

**Determinism and six Postgres proofs.** `backend/tests/test_w202_cold_replay_postgres.py` uses only `assetops_w202c_test` or another private `*_test` DB. Copy required gitignored `var/sites` fixture files into its isolated work directory and remove that copy in teardown. Compare a second clean run's canonical envelope and Batch bytes and each manifest hash; hold mutable receipt IDs stable only through the test fixture controls just stated.

**Rule 8 and final gates.** `cold_replay.py`, its Postgres reader and `cold_replay_api.py` issue only `SELECT`; `postgres_evidence_ledger.py` remains the sole writer of `accepted_evidence`, `evidence_rejection`, `ingestion_disposition`, `ingestion_batch`, `evidence_publication` and `batch_publication_link`. # W2-03 independent review - cold assessment, episodes, Findings, four cold screens

Reviewer: fresh Claude reviewer (not the author). Revision reviewed: `ac967654` (diff `a3ac189c..ac967654`, 23 non-merge commits).
Inputs: `tasks/W2-03-cold-assessment.md` (incl. plan-review fixes M1, L1-L3), `.agent/W2-03-build-spec.md` (incl. `D-2026-10-18-w2-03-answers` and the q2 amendment), `.agent/W2-03-review-packet.md` (deviations 1-21), captures in `.agent/W2-03-evidence/`.

## Verdict: RETURN

Two Medium findings, both small to fix on the branch. The rules, migration, worker, read contract and v69 structure are otherwise sound. Deviations 16 (withheld Cooling subtab and Recommendation tab) and 18 (W1-vocabulary strings bound by the mock and JSON) are **pending with the Architect** and are not re-argued here. Deviation 1 (work window) is now confirmed by the q2 amendment.

## Findings

1. **Medium - contradiction on the failed backup chain.** `frontend/src/sites/coldAssessmentWords.ts:72` (`PROBE: ["Chamber temperature after recovery", ...]`), used by `frontend/src/sites/ColdAssessmentViews.tsx:474-478` `establishes()`. The `coldbackup` stocked capture (backup-failed replay; start failed, transfer missing, bus unpowered, compressor off) shows "Chamber temperature after recovery" under *What it establishes* in the Probe row. There was no recovery, so the page contradicts itself. The packet's self-check fixed the GENERATOR, BUS and COMPRESSOR rows but missed this one. This is on the recurring-defect list ("no contradictions").
   **Fix:** in `establishes()`, return "Chamber temperature after recovery" for PROBE only when `coolingRunning(chain)` is true. Otherwise return a neutral phrase such as "Chamber temperature after the compressor record". Add a `coldRoutes.test.tsx` assertion that the backup-failed fixture does not contain "after recovery". Recapture `coldbackup-stocked` at 1440 and 390.

2. **Medium - plan-review fix L1 / spec "Negative accepted-evidence cases must enter by authenticated gateway Batch V2" is only partly met.** The SUSPECT and equal-event-ID cases go through intake (`backend/tests/test_w203_cold_replay_postgres.py:296-328`), and missing-transfer comes from the backup-failed replay. The **clock-skew/unattested** and **unbound-role** cases exist only as pure-rule tests over hand-edited `ColdEvidence` (`backend/tests/test_w203_cold_rules.py:244-265`). So the adapter mapping that decides them on real rows, `postgres_assessment_store.py:890-900` (`in_force` binding check and `attested` attestation match), has no negative end-to-end proof. If `attested` were always true, unattested readings would support claims and no test would fail. The packet does not record this as a deviation.
   **Fix:** add an intake-driven replay test. Record the SYSTEM attestation for every Lab source except the chamber-probe source, or give that source an attestation with `valid_to` before 19:25. Then assert that the affected readings link as `EXCLUDED`/`CLOCK_UNATTESTED`, never `SUPPORT`, and that coverage and gaps drop accordingly. For unbound role: either drive an accepted record outside its binding's effective period through intake and assert `EXCLUDED`/`UNBOUND`, or, if S2 cannot accept such a record, state that in the packet as a deviation with the reason.

3. **Low - wording run-on.** `ColdAssessmentViews.tsx:626` renders "Above policy At least 45 min" in the Backup event chains *Outcome for service* cell, because `range()` returns a capitalised phrase. **Fix:** lower-case the range here or join with " Â· ".

4. **Low - prominence of the result chip.** `ColdAssessmentViews.tsx:628` gives every chain result a neutral `Badge`. The mock's Result chip is red for a failed start. **Fix:** use the danger tone for `OBSERVED_FAILURE`, warning for `CONTRADICTORY`, neutral otherwise.

5. **Low - test gap.** M1 late-reading asks for "first high readings at 19:15 and 19:25" at the 20:05 cutoff. `test_w203_cold_replay_postgres.py:132-136` asserts only the 19:15 anchor. **Fix:** also assert the 19:25 point is present and HIGH, and that 19:20 is absent.

6. **Low - test gap.** No test covers a LOW-direction excursion (Q9 peak = minimum, Q4 durations). I checked it by script against `assess_excursion`: a 2-4 Â°C band with readings 1.5/1.2/0.9/1.1/1.4 gives LOW, peak 0.9, duration {20, 30}, BOUNDED. That is correct. **Fix:** add this as `test_w203_cold_rules.py::test_low_excursion_peak_is_minimum`.

7. **Low - stale docstring.** `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/cold_reads.py:4-5` names `analytics/adapters/postgres_cold_reads.py:ColdReadService`. That file does not exist (deviation 7 moved the class into `postgres_assessment_store.py`). **Fix:** correct the path.

No finding on assertions: no existing assertion was weakened. The edits elsewhere are 0021 to 0022 head bumps, plus the W2-00 "no dispatch job" filter that excludes `cold_chain`, which the q2 amendment confirmed (interpretation 5).

Panel set, order, titles, grid and prominence match v69: 3 panels (with the mock's metric-card row and footnote), 4, 3 and 3. The state is said once, in the strip. ## Residual risk (not in the packet)

- `postgres_evidence_ledger.py:650`: cold job IDs come from `gen_random_uuid()` only so the replay fixtures' pinned `uuid4` sequence does not shift. The production code path is bent around test ID pinning. It is harmless, but the next producer that pins IDs the same way will be fragile.
- `_cold_revision` and `cold_persist` treat a basis that returns to an earlier value (A, then B, then A) as already held. No FindingRevision is appended, contrary to the commit-3 sentence "a returned old basis ... must append a new FindingRevision". A forced episode insert would also hit `cold_episode_revision_basis_unique`. Under DEFAULT selection this cannot happen, because `known_at` (inside the basis) only grows. It becomes reachable only if an explicit or older-cutoff path ever selects.
- Deviation 14 (cold IDs drilling into the existing Evidence Analysis view) is still unverified, as the packet states.
# W2-02 review - cold producer, Lab scenarios, gateway v4, Lab cold setup route, replay

Reviewer: fresh independent Claude reviewer (not the author). Revision reviewed: `8668396f` (code `7b0c9f79`).

## Verdict: RETURN

There are two Medium findings. Both are small fixes on the branch. Deviation 1 is still the separate known
blocker with the Architect, so after the fixes the verdict becomes **ACCEPT-pending-deviation-1**. Everything
else matches the spec, and I verified it as listed below.

## Findings

1. **Medium (acceptance gap): a mismatched or half-cold profile pair still creates a Draft.**
   Location: `backend/assetops_backend/runs/service.py:429-430`, the legacy branch of `create_draft_run`.
   The spec says: "Unknown profile, pack, observation version or mismatch refuses before Draft". The packet marks
   this "Met", but it is not:
   - `GET /run-profiles` now offers `cold-thermal/1` and `cold-publication/1` to the legacy setup form.
   - I ran a probe through `RunSetupService` (fixtures from `test_w202_cold_run_identity`). A legacy scenario with
     the cold pair returned a persisted `BLOCKED` Draft (STATE_NOT_SUPPORTED). So did `cold-thermal/1` with a
     legacy publication profile. Neither was refused.
   - Neither run can execute: the host's `execution_path` and `verify_cold_inputs` refuse it. The failure is
     safe, but it breaks the contract and leaves an orphan Draft.

   Fix: in the legacy branch, after resolving the profiles, refuse `VERSION_UNAVAILABLE` when either
   `model.model_profile_id == "cold-thermal"` or `publication.publication_profile_id == "cold-publication"`.
   Suggested copy: "Cold-chain profiles run only from a cold-scenario/1 document. Profiles are not substituted."
   Add a test to `test_w202_cold_run_identity.py` for both pairs, asserting `store.written == []`.

2. **Medium: the Cold run setup screen states its state more than once.**
   Location: `frontend/src/shell/ColdRunSetupFrame.tsx:286/289/308` (Cold scenarios panel), `:406` (the reason
   beside **Create draft run**) and `:344` (the Foundation version fact).
   - With no Site chosen, "Choose a site to see its cold scenarios." renders twice: in the panel and beside the
     button.
   - The empty and unavailable copies also render twice.
   - Once a choice is made, the basis-loading or basis-problem reason renders both in the "Foundation version"
     fact and beside the button.

   The coldRunSetup tests only read the `data-cold-choice-state` node, so they do not catch this. It breaks the
   owner's say-it-once rule this dispatch asked me to check.

   Fix:
   - Render the button-side reason only when the Cold scenarios panel does not already show it. Suppress it for
     the choose-site, empty and unavailable states, and keep the button disabled.
   - Make the Foundation version fact show the version, or "Not available yet", never `blockedReason`.
   - Add a test asserting each of the three copies appears exactly once (`getAllByText(...).length === 1`).

3. **Low: a raw ID in the choice label and a raw enum in refusals.**
   Location: `ColdRunSetupFrame.tsx:443` renders `Normal (cold-normal)`, and `:368` shows `Reason code: X`.
   The spec requires the scenario ID and profile identities as read-only facts on this developer surface. Those
   facts are acceptable, but the label does not need the ID repeated.
   Fix: label with `choice.display_name` only. Optionally drop the reason-code line, since the refusal message
   already says what is wrong.

4. **Low: the generator's STARTING to STOPPED move on grid restore skips the transition guard.**
   Location: `simulator/assetops_simulator/packs/cold.py:307`. The restore branch sets `generator="STOPPED"` from
   `STARTING` without calling `check_move`, and `_GENERATOR_MOVES` does not contain `("STARTING","STOPPED")`.
   Fix: add that move and call `check_move("generator", s.generator, "STOPPED")`.

5. **Low: the cold replay reader is wider than SELECT-only.**
   Location: `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/composition.py:41`. `build_cold_replay_reader` returns the whole
   `PostgresEvidenceLedger`, writer methods included. The service only calls `cold_replay_*`, and the static rule-8
   guard holds.
   Fix: return the object typed as the `ColdReplayReader` protocol, or a two-method facade.

6. **Low: a cold execution error escapes as an HTTP 500.**
   Location: `host/lab_execution.py:2295`. `_start_cold` does not catch a `PhysicallyUnacceptable` raised during
   the window, for example valid Foundation values that drive T outside [-40,60]. It surfaces as a 500, not as a
   refusal. The spec does not name a kind for this.
   Fix: map it to `LabControlRefused` before any Stage output, or record it as a deliberate deferral.

+## Retro rulings (2026-10-18)
+
+`D-2026-10-18-w2-retro-rulings` and `.agent/W2-retro-rulings.md` make the W2-02 packet's remaining implementation choices part of this contract. Manifest fields use committed batch payload hashes, source-time event order with receipt-order ties, accepted `UNAVAILABLE` counts, and first qualifying start/transfer events; manifest writing remains opt-in. Replay checks access, owner, parameters, Site, then run, with out-of-scope runs 404 and unavailable persisted lineage 503. Deterministic UUID, gateway epoch and clock control is test-only. The schema-6 cold private artifact is owned by the existing `YamlExecutionArtifacts` store; the Draft has frozen `run_identity_sha256` and canonical empty-series digest. `PUBLICATION_REFUSED` is a host-only terminal failure, excluded from the simulator reachability guard but proved in host tests. The composition leaf supplies the cold pack, and the replay reader exposes only its two read methods.
+
+Cold Lab setup splits a profile at its last `/`, reads the effective basis at the document start, clears the result on Site change, and offers “Set up a cold-chain run” from Lab home. Show each choose-site, empty, unavailable or basis-loading reason once; the Foundation version fact shows a version or “Not available”, while the separate state message gives the refusal. A scenario choice uses only its display name, with its ID in a labelled fact. A legacy scenario carrying either cold profile refuses before Draft. Restore from STARTING to STOPPED uses the guarded transition. A physically unacceptable frozen execution refuses `FROZEN_RUN_NOT_RECONSTRUCTIBLE` before Stage. These rulings add no cold session channel or product evidence writer.
 
 Decision `D-2026-10-17-w2-02-buildspec`. This is the executable contract for `tasks/W2-02-cold-producer.md`; later shorthand in that packet yields to this spec. No product data may be derived from private world state except through the public report adapter. Implement in the commit order at the end.

+## Retro rulings (2026-10-18)
+
+`D-2026-10-18-w2-retro-rulings` and `.agent/W2-retro-rulings.md` close the review packet's remaining choices. The already ruled work-window bridge, window-end freshness, Cooling and Recommendation rows, and approved wording exceptions below stand. The SQL selected-read class stays in `postgres_assessment_store.py` under the driver guard; pure rules stay in `cold_reads.py`. With neither bound supplied, select the latest settled cold window at the cutoff; with exactly one bound, return 422. A selected run covers the requested window and has cutoff at or before `known_at`. Overlapping PENDING, LEASED or FAILED cold work within the 10-minute bridge is pending. A changed cursor basis is 409; broken selected storage is 503. MIXED source provenance remains visible and cannot authorize a Finding. The default disclosure seam does not create a source permission claim.
+
+**Correction gate: cold Analysis links.** The exact existing Evidence Analysis `record` and `assessment` destinations must be exercised with selected cold IDs in an integration test that proves they render the intended record or revision with Site/window/cutoff scope. Only then render those destinations as links. Until that proof, show labelled exact references with “Analysis view not available for this cold record”; preserve supported Finding and evidence links. An unverified route is not a user-visible destination.
+
+**Correction gate: mock row label.** Restore **Loading / batch** on the cold Temperature context row, with **Not established** when no record backs it. The capture settling detector must identify actual loading-state elements or exact loading sentences, so ordinary row content containing “Loading” cannot be mistaken for a pending page. Do not change the v6.9 label to satisfy tooling.
+
+For an unknown-kind refused cold Finding, retain the mock slots with refusal and in-slot gaps, without suggesting an established kind. Manual recovery without a log is “Not recorded” and has no Incident link. The chart axis spans actual selected policy and observations with one-degree padding; the mock illustration's numeric ticks are not claim data. Keep these rules in the four screen fixtures and 1440/390 captures. The reviewed fixes also bind: probe “after recovery” only with running cooling, intake-driven `CLOCK_UNATTESTED` exclusion, pure-only unbound-role defence when S2 refuses intake, the separated duration phrase, result-chip tones, late-reading and LOW-peak proofs, and the corrected read-class docstring.
